, we
need to show that the map

\tmop{dR}_{(\Gamma_{A[X]}(Y)\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}[X])/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}\rightarrow\tmop{dR}_{(\tmop{id}_{A^{\prime\prime}[X]}\of A^{\prime\prime}[X]\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime}[X],0)/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}

is an equivalence. Note that the constructed map

(\Gamma_{A[X]}(Y)\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}[X],\gamma)\rightarrow(\tmop{id}_{A^{\prime\prime}[X]}\of A^{\prime\prime}[X]\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime}[X],0)

in

factors as

(\Gamma_{A[X]}(Y)\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}[X],\gamma)\xrightarrow{\alpha}(A[X]\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}[X],\gamma)\xrightarrow{\beta}(\tmop{id}_{A^{\prime\prime}[X]}\of A^{\prime\prime}[X]\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime}[X],0)

Thus it suffices to show that both maps
\alpha
and
\beta
induces

\tmop{dR}_{\cdummy/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)}\of\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}_{/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}
. Note
that
(A[X]\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}[X],\gamma)\in\tmop{dRCon}^{0}
,

\tmop{dR}_{\alpha/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)}

[

07LC
]
.

It remains to show that

is also an equivalence. For this, we need to resolve

(A[X]\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}[X],\gamma)

A=\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}[X^{\prime}]}(Y^{\prime})
and

. The key point is
that we can resolve

A

[

. For every

(\Gamma_{A[X]}(Z)\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}[X],\gamma)

\tmop{dRCon}^{0}
, and the

\tmop{dR}_{(A[X]\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}[X])/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}\rightarrow\tmop{dR}_{(\Gamma_{A[X]}(Z)\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}[X])/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}
(functorial in

) is an

[
Sta21
,
Tag
07LC
]
. It follows that
\tmop{dR}_{\beta/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)}
is indeed an equivalence.

In view of

2.63
, we define the derived crystalline cohomology
functor which corresponds to the

-invariant functor
\tmop{dR}_{\cdummy/\cdummy}

Definition 4.20
.

The
\tmdfn
derived crystalline cohomology
functor
\tmop{CrysCoh}\of\tmop{CrysCon}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}
is defined to be the composite
\tmop{CrysCon}\rightarrow\tmop{dRCon}\xrightarrow{\tmop{dR}_{\cdummy/\cdummy}}\tmop{CAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}
.

{notation}

We will denote the derived crystalline cohomology
of
((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma),A^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow R)\in\tmop{CrysCon}
by
\tmop{CrysCoh}_{R/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)}

(or
\tmop{CrysCoh}_{R/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}
even

\tmop{CrysCon}_{R/A}
when there is no ambiguity).

Now we show that

Proposition 4.21
.

The derived crystalline cohomology
functor
\tmop{CrysCon}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}
preserves
small colimits.

Proof 4.22
.

The functor
\tmop{CrysCon}\rightarrow\tmop{dRCon}
preserves sifted
colimits and
\tmop{dR}_{\cdummy/\cdummy}\of\tmop{dRCon}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}
preserves small colimits, it follows that the
derived crystalline cohomology functor
\tmop{CrysCoh}
preserves sifted
colimits. By
Proposition

A.14
, it remains to show that

\nobracket\tmop{CrysCoh}|_{\tmop{CrysCon}^{0}}
preserves finite
coproducts. The point is that every
(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}[X^{\prime}]}(Y^{\prime})\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}[X^{\prime}],\mathbb{Z}[X^{\prime}]\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}[X,X^{\prime}])\in\tmop{CrysCon}^{0}
lifts to
(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}[X^{\prime}]}(Y^{\prime})\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}[X^{\prime}])\rightarrow(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}[X,X^{\prime}]}(Y,Y^{\prime})\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}[X,X^{\prime}])\in\tmop{dRCon}^{0}
, the
functor
\tmop{dRCon}^{0}\rightarrow\tmop{CrysCon}^{0}
preserves finite
coproducts, and the functor
\tmop{dR}_{\cdummy/\cdummy}
preserves finite
coproducts.

Now we apply the discussions in
Section

2.3
to deduce some formal
properties. First, by
Remark

2.31
, we have

Corollary 4.23
.

The derived crystalline cohomology is
compatible with base change. More precisely, let
((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{A}),A^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow R)\in\tmop{CrysCon}
and let
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{A})\rightarrow(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{B})
be a map of animated PD-pairs. Then the canonical map

\tmop{CrysCoh}_{R/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{A})}\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}B\longrightarrow\tmop{CrysCoh}_{(R\otimes_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\mathbb{L}}B^{\prime\prime})/(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{B})}

is an equivalence.

Remark 4.24
.

Let
R
be a (finitely generated) polynomial
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebra. Then by
the
p
-completed derived crystalline cohomology of
R
with respect to the
PD-pair
(\mathbb{Z}_{p},(p))
is equivalent to the usual (
p
-completed)
crystalline cohomology of
R
with respect to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. In fact, later
(
Propositions

4.104
and
4.107
), we will show
that the same
p
-completed comparison holds for quasisyntomic

\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebras
R
(and in particular, for smooth

\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebras). However, our non-completed derived crystalline
cohomology is not necessarily
p
-complete.

Next, by
Remark

2.35
, we have

Corollary 4.25
.

The derived crystalline cohomology is symmetric
monoidal. More precisely, let
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{A})\in\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}
and let
A\rightarrow R
,
A\rightarrow S
be two maps of animated rings. Then the canonical map

\tmop{CrysCoh}_{R/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}\tmop{CrysCoh}_{S/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}\longrightarrow\tmop{CrysCoh}_{(R\otimes_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\mathbb{L}}S)/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}

is an equivalence.

Finally, by
Remark

2.38
, we have

Corollary 4.26
.

The derived crystalline cohomology is
transitive. More precisely, let
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{A})\rightarrow(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{B})
be a map of animated
PD-pairs, and let
B^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow R
be a map of animated rings. Then the
canonical map

\tmop{CrysCoh}_{R/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}\otimes_{\tmop{CrysCoh}_{B^{\prime\prime}/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}}^{\mathbb{L}}B\longrightarrow\tmop{CrysCoh}_{R/(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})}

is an equivalence, where the map
\tmop{CrysCoh}_{B^{\prime\prime}/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}\rightarrow B
is
\tmop{CrysCoh}_{B^{\prime\prime}/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}\rightarrow\tmop{CrysCoh}_{B^{\prime\prime}/(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})}\simeq B
.

Remark 4.27
.

In particular, if we take
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{A})=(\mathbb{Z},0,0)
in
Corollary

4.26
, we see that, fix an
animated PD-pair
(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{B})
, any derived
crystalline cohomology
\tmop{CrysCoh}_{R/(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})}
is
completely determined by the derived de Rham cohomology
\tmop{dR}_{R/\mathbb{Z}}
. However, without the theory of derived crystalline
cohomology, we do not know how to construct the map
\tmop{dR}_{B^{\prime\prime}/\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow B
in terms of the PD-structure on
B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime}
.

4.2.
Filtrations

In this subsection, we
will define the
\tmdfn
Hodge filtration on the derived de Rham cohomology
and show that most of our previous discussions are compatible with the Hodge
filtration. Furthermore, in characteristic
p
, we will define the

\tmdfn
conjugate filtration, which is of technical importance to control
the cohomology. We start with the definition of the Hodge filtration.

Definition 4.28

(cf.
[
BO78
, §6.13]
)
.

Let
(A,I,\gamma)\rightarrow(B,J,\delta)
be a map of PD-pairs such
that
\Omega_{(B,J)/(A,I)}^{1}
is a flat
B
-module. The
\tmdfn
Hodge
filtration
\tmop{Fil}_{H}^{\ast}
on the de Rham complex
(\Omega_{(B,J)/(A,I)}^{\ast},\mathd)
is given by the differential graded ideals

\tmop{Fil}_{H}^{m}\Omega_{(B,J)/(A,I)}^{\ast}\assign J^{\left[m-\mathord{\ast}\right]}\Omega_{(B,J)/(A,I)}^{\ast}\subseteq\Omega_{(B,J)/(A,I)}^{\ast}
.

\construction

As CDGAs give rise to

\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-
\mathbb{Z}
-algebras, (nonnegatively) filtered CDGAs
give rise to (nonnegatively) filtered

\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-
\mathbb{Z}
-algebras. Moreover, the truncation map

(\Omega_{(B,J)/(A,I)}^{\ast},\mathd)\rightarrow B
is a map of filtered
CDGAs, which gives rise to a map of filtered

\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-
\mathbb{Z}
-algebras. Thus we get a functor

\tmop{dRCon}^{0}\rightarrow\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\geq 0}(\mathbb{Z})))
.

Definition 4.29
.

The
\tmdfn
Hodge-filtered derived de Rham
cohomology functor
\tmop{Fil}_{H}^{\ast}\tmop{dR}_{\cdummy/\cdummy}\of\tmop{dRCon}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\geq 0}(\mathbb{Z}))

together with a canonical map
\tmop{Fil}_{H}^{\ast}\tmop{dR}_{(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}\rightarrow\tmop{Fil}_{\tmop{PD}}^{\ast}B
is defined to be the left derived functor
(
Proposition

A.14
) of the functor
\tmop{dRCon}^{0}\rightarrow\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\geq 0}(\mathbb{Z})))
in
Construction
4.2
, where

\tmop{Fil}_{\tmop{PD}}^{\ast}B
is an abbreviation of

\tmop{Fil}_{\tmop{PD}}^{\ast}(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime},\gamma)

(
Definition

3.92
).

Most of properties in
Section

4.1
hold with a similar proof:

Lemma 4.30
.

The composite functor

\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\tmop{CAlg}^{\tmop{an}})\rightarrow\tmop{dRCon}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\geq 0}(\mathbb{Z})),(A\rightarrow B)\mapsto\tmop{Fil}_{H}^{\ast}\tmop{dR}_{(\tmop{id}_{B}\of B\rightarrow B,0)/(\tmop{id}_{A}\of A\rightarrow A,0)}

is equivalent to the classical Hodge-filtered derived de Rham cohomology
functor
(A\rightarrow B)\mapsto\tmop{Fil}_{H}^{\ast}\tmop{dR}_{B/A}
.

Lemma 4.31
.

The map in
Lemma

4.12
admits a natural enrichment, that is to
say, a map
\tmop{Fil}_{\tmop{PD}}^{\ast}A\rightarrow\tmop{Fil}_{H}^{\ast}\tmop{dR}_{(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}
of
functors
\tmop{dRCon}\rightrightarrows\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\geq 0}(\mathbb{Z}))
.

Lemma 4.32
.

The Hodge-filtered derived de Rham cohomology functor
\tmop{dRCon}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\geq 0}(\mathbb{Z}))
preserves small
colimits.

This allows us to define the Hodge-filtration on the derived crystalline
cohomology, due to the following proposition, which follows from the proof of

Proposition

4.18
by replacing the Poincaré lemma by the filtered
Poincaré lemma, cf.
[
BO78
, Thm 6.13]
:

Proposition 4.33
.

The map
\tmop{Fil}_{H}^{\ast}\tmop{dR}_{(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime},\delta)/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)}\rightarrow\tmop{Fil}_{H}^{\ast}\tmop{dR}_{(\tmop{id}_{B^{\prime\prime}},0)/(A\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)}
of functors

\tmop{dRCon}\rightrightarrows\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\geq 0}(\mathbb{Z}))
induced by the counit map associated to
((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\rightarrow(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime},\delta))\in\tmop{dRCon}
is an equivalence. In other words, the
Hodge-filtered de Rham cohomology functor
\tmop{dRCon}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\geq 0}(\mathbb{Z}))
is
(\tmop{dRCon}\rightarrow\tmop{CrysCon})
-invariant (
Definition

2.64
).

Definition 4.34
.

The
\tmdfn
Hodge-filtered derived crystalline cohomology functor

\tmop{Fil}_{H}^{\ast}\tmop{CrysCoh}\of\tmop{CrysCon}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\geq 0}(\mathbb{Z}))
is defined to be the
composite
\tmop{CrysCon}\rightarrow\tmop{dRCon}\xrightarrow{\tmop{Fil}_{H}^{\ast}\tmop{dR}_{\cdummy/\cdummy}}\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\geq 0}(\mathbb{Z}))
.

Proposition 4.35
.

The Hodge-filtered derived crystalline cohomology functor
\tmop{CrysCon}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\geq 0}(\mathbb{Z}))
preserves small
colimits.

Similar to

Corollaries

4.23
,
4.25
and
4.26
, we
have

Corollary 4.36
.

The Hodge-filtered derived crystalline
cohomology is compatible with base change. More precisely, let
((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{A}),A^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow R)\in\tmop{CrysCon}

and let
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{A})\rightarrow(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{B})
be a map of animated PD-pairs. Then the
canonical map

\tmop{Fil}_{H}\tmop{CrysCoh}_{R/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{A})}\otimes_{\tmop{Fil}_{\tmop{PD}}A}^{\mathbb{L}}\tmop{Fil}_{\tmop{PD}}B\longrightarrow\tmop{Fil}_{H}\tmop{CrysCoh}_{(R\otimes_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\mathbb{L}}B^{\prime\prime})/(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{B})}

is an equivalence.

Corollary 4.37
.

The derived crystalline cohomology is
symmetric monoidal. More precisely, let
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{A})\in\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}
and let
A\rightarrow R
,
A\rightarrow S
be two maps of animated rings. Then the canonical map

\tmop{Fil}_{H}\tmop{CrysCoh}_{R/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\tmop{Fil}_{H}\tmop{CrysCoh}_{S/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}\rightarrow\tmop{Fil}_{H}\tmop{CrysCoh}_{(R\otimes_{A^{\prime\prime}}^{\mathbb{L}}S)/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}

is an equivalence, where the tensor product on the left is relative to

\tmop{Fil}_{\tmop{PD}}A
.

Corollary 4.38
.

The derived crystalline cohomology is
transitive. More precisely, let
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{A})\rightarrow(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{B})
be a map of animated
PD-pairs, and let
B^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow R
be a map of animated rings. Then the
canonical map

\tmop{Fil}_{H}\tmop{CrysCoh}_{R/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}\otimes_{\tmop{Fil}_{H}\tmop{CrysCoh}_{B^{\prime\prime}/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}}^{\mathbb{L}}\tmop{Fil}_{\tmop{PD}}B\longrightarrow\tmop{Fil}_{H}\tmop{CrysCoh}_{R/(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})}

is an equivalence, where the map
\tmop{Fil}_{H}\tmop{CrysCoh}_{B^{\prime\prime}/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}\rightarrow B
is equivalent to the map

\tmop{Fil}_{H}\tmop{CrysCoh}_{B^{\prime\prime}/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}\rightarrow\tmop{Fil}_{H}\tmop{CrysCoh}_{B^{\prime\prime}/(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})}\simeq\tmop{Fil}_{\tmop{PD}}B.

Now we come to the characteristic
p>0
case. We start with an analysis of
the Frobenius map on an animated PD-
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-pair. Let
(A,I,\gamma)\in\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{st}}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
be an animated
PD-
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-pair of the form
\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[X]}(Y)\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{p}[X]
. We also have similar definitions for

\tmop{dRCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}},\tmop{dRCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{0}
and

\tmop{CrysCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}},\tmop{CrysCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{0}
, and a
parallel theory for
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-stuff. We first point out that, by

Corollary

4.23
along with the proof of

Lemma

2.45
(to compare with

Lemmas

3.55
and
3.56
), we have

Lemma 4.39
.

The derived crystalline cohomology

\tmop{CrysCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}(D(\mathbb{F}_{p}))

fits into the commutative diagram

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\tmop{CrysCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}&\longrightarrow&\tmop{CrysCon}\\
\longdownarrow&&\longdownarrow\\
\tmop{CAlg}(D(\mathbb{F}_{p}))&\longrightarrow&\tmop{CAlg}(D(\mathbb{Z}))\end{array}

of
\infty
-categories, where the horizontal arrows are forgetful
functors. The same for the derived de Rham cohomology. Furthermore, this
diagram is left-adjointable (roughly speaking, if we replace the horizontal
arrows by their left adjoints, it is still a commutative diagram of

\infty
-categories).

Then the Frobenius map
\varphi_{A}\of A\rightarrow A
factors uniquely
through the quotient map
A\twoheadrightarrow A/I
, which gives rise to a
map
A/I\rightarrow A
. It then follows from
Proposition

A.14

that

Lemma 4.40
.

For any animated PD-
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-pair
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\in\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
, the Frobenius map
\varphi_{A}\of A\rightarrow A
factors functorially through the map
A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}
, which
gives rise to the a map
A^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow A
, denoted by
\varphi_{(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)}
or
\varphi_{A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}}

when there is no ambiguity (when
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)
comes
from a PD-
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-pair
(A,I,\gamma)
, it will also be denoted by

\varphi_{(A,I,\gamma)}
or
\varphi_{(A,I)}
).

Now we point out that in the
\tmop{char}p
-case, the de Rham complex is
“Frobenius-linear” (compare with
Definition

3.72
):

\construction

Let
(A,I,\gamma)\rightarrow(B,J,\delta)
be an object in
\tmop{dRCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{0}
. Each graded
piece
\Omega_{(B,J,\delta)/(A,I,\gamma)}^{i}
admits a natural
B
-module
structure therefore also a
\varphi_{(A,I)}^{\ast}(B/J)
-module structure
induced by the map
\varphi_{(A,I)}^{\ast}(B/J)\assign(B/J)\otimes_{A/I,\varphi_{(A,I)}}^{\mathbb{L}}A\rightarrow B
, the linearization of

\varphi_{(B,J)}\of B/J\rightarrow B
. Furthermore, the differential

\mathd
is
\varphi_{(A,I)}^{\ast}(B/J)
-linear, which makes the de Rham
complex
(\Omega_{(B,J,\delta)/(A,I,\gamma)}^{\ast},\mathd)
a

\varphi_{(A,I)}^{\ast}(B/J)
-CDGA. In other words, there is a map

\varphi_{(A,I)}^{\ast}(B/J)\rightarrow(\Omega_{(B,J,\delta)/(A,I,\gamma)}^{\ast},\mathd)
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-CDGAs, where
\varphi_{(A,I)}^{\ast}(B/J)
is concentrated in degree
0
.

\construction

Let
(A,I,\gamma)\rightarrow(B,J,\delta)
be an object in
\tmop{dRCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{0}
. The derived de
Rham cohomology
\tmop{dR}_{(B,J,\delta)/(A,I,\gamma)}
is computed by
the de Rham complex
(\Omega_{(B,J,\delta)/(A,I,\gamma)}^{\ast},\mathd)
. The Whitehead tower
(\tau_{\geq n}\tmop{dR}_{(B,J,\delta)/(A,I,\gamma)})_{n\in(\mathbb{Z},\geq)}
defines a nonpositive
22
22

22

In the
literature, the conjugate filtration is increasing. We make it decreasing by
negating the sign.
exhaustive filtration, thus the map
\varphi_{(A,I)}^{\ast}(B/J)\rightarrow(\Omega_{(B,J,\delta)/(A,I,\gamma)}^{\ast},\mathd)
is a map of filtered
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-CDGAs (where

\varphi_{(A,I)}^{\ast}(B/J)
is trivially filtered), which gives rise to
a map of filtered
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebras, thus a
functor
\tmop{dRCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{0}\rightarrow\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\leq 0}(\mathbb{F}_{p})))
.

Definition 4.41
.

The
\tmdfn
conjugate-filtered derived de Rham cohomology functor

\tmop{Fil}_{\tmop{conj}}^{\ast}\tmop{dR}_{\cdummy/\cdummy}\of\tmop{dRCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\leq 0}(\mathbb{F}_{p}))
along with the
\tmdfn
structure map
\varphi_{(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}^{\ast}(B^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow\tmop{Fil}_{\tmop{conj}}^{\ast}\tmop{dR}_{(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime},\delta)/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)}
is defined to be the left

Proposition

A.14
) of the functor

\tmop{dRCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{0}\rightarrow\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\leq 0}(\mathbb{F}_{p})))
in
Construction
4.2
.

It follows either from
Propositions

A.14
and
2.51

or the fact that
\tmop{Pair}^{\tmop{an}}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\tmop{Pair}^{\tmop{st}})\subseteq\mathcal{P}(\tmop{Pair}^{\tmop{st}})
is
stable under filtered colimits (
Proposition

A.10
) that

Lemma 4.42
.

The conjugate filtration on the derived de
Rham cohomology is exhaustive.

We now prove the corresponding results of
Section

4.1
for the
conjugate filtration.

Lemma 4.43
.

The conjugate-filtered derived de Rham cohomology functor

\tmop{dRCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\leq 0}(\mathbb{F}_{p}))
preserves small colimits (note that so does the functor

\tmop{dRCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}},((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\rightarrow(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime},\delta))\mapsto\varphi_{(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}^{\ast}(B^{\prime\prime})
).

Proof 4.44
.

First, we note that, for any connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
A
, the
Whitehead-tower functor
D(A)\rightarrow\tmop{DF}(A),M\mapsto(\tau_{\geq n}M)_{n\in(\mathbb{Z},\geq)}
is canonically lax symmetric
monoidal (recall that
\tmop{DF}(A)
is endowed with the Day convolution).
We give an informal description: given
M,N\in D(A)
, for all
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the canonical map
\tau_{\geq m}M\rightarrow M
and

\tau_{\geq n}N\rightarrow N
gives rise to a map
(\tau_{\geq m}M)\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}(\tau_{\geq n}N)\rightarrow M\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}N
. Since
(\tau_{\geq m}M)\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}(\tau_{\geq n}N)
is
(m+n)
-connective, this gives rise to a map

(\tau_{\geq m}M)\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}(\tau_{\geq n}N)\rightarrow\tau_{\geq m+n}(M\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}N)
. Assembling these maps, we
get the lax symmetric monoidal structure. Next, when
A
is given by a
field, in particular,
A=\mathbb{F}_{p}
, the structure above is in fact
symmetric monoidal, since
(\tau_{\geq m}M)\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}(\tau_{\geq n}N)\rightarrow\tau_{\geq m+n}(M\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}N)
is an equivalence for all
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Now recall that in a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category, finite coproducts
of commutative algebra objects are given by tensor products. It follows from

Lemma

4.14
that the conjugate-filtered derived de Rham
cohomology functor
\tmop{dRCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\leq 0}(\mathbb{F}_{p}))
is the left derived functor of a
finite-coproduct-preserving functor, and then the result follows from

Proposition

A.14
.

Note that by the divided power Poincaré’s lemma

[
Sta21
,
Tag
07LC
]
, the conjugate filtration on the divided power
polynomial algebra is trivial. The proof of
Proposition

4.18
leads to

Proposition 4.45
.

The natural transformation
\tmop{Fil}_{\tmop{conj}}^{\ast}\tmop{dR}_{(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime},\delta)/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)}\rightarrow\tmop{Fil}_{\tmop{conj}}^{\ast}\tmop{dR}_{(\tmop{id}_{B^{\prime\prime}},0)/(A\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)}
of functors
\tmop{dRCon}\rightrightarrows\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\leq 0}(\mathbb{Z}))
induced by the counit map
associated to
((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\rightarrow(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime},\delta))\in\tmop{dRCon}
is an equivalence. In
other words, the conjugate-filtered de Rham cohomology functor
\tmop{dRCon}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\leq 0}(\mathbb{Z}))
is
(\tmop{dRCon}\rightarrow\tmop{CrysCon})
-invariant (
Definition

2.64
) (note that so is
the functor
\tmop{dRCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}},((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\rightarrow(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime},\delta))\mapsto\varphi_{(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}^{\ast}(B^{\prime\prime})
).

Definition 4.46
.

The
\tmdfn
conjugate-filtered derived
crystalline cohomology functor
\tmop{Fil}_{\tmop{conj}}^{\ast}\tmop{CrysCoh}\of\tmop{CrysCon}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\leq 0}(\mathbb{F}_{p}))
along with the
\tmdfn
structure map
\varphi_{(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)}^{\ast}(R)\rightarrow\tmop{CrysCoh}_{R/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)}
is defined to be the composite

\tmop{CrysCon}\rightarrow\tmop{dRCon}\rightarrow\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\leq 0}(\mathbb{F}_{p})))
, where the later functor
is the conjugate-filtered derived de Rham cohomology functor combined with
the structure map.

By
Lemma

4.42
, we have

Lemma 4.47
.

The conjugate filtration on the
derived crystalline cohomology is exhaustive.

Similar to
Proposition

4.21
, we have

Proposition 4.48
.

The conjugate-filtered derived crystalline cohomology functor

\tmop{CrysCon}\rightarrow\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\leq 0}(\mathbb{F}_{p}))

preserves small colimits.

Now we analyze the associated graded pieces of the conjugate filtration. Let

(A,I,\gamma)\rightarrow(B,J,\delta)
be an element in
\tmop{dRCon}^{0}
.
We recall the inverse
23
23

23

A priori, the “inverse” Cartier map
C^{-1}

is not defined to be the inverse of a map, but just defined to be a map.

Cartier map
C^{-1}\of\varphi_{(A,I)}^{\ast}(\Omega_{(B/J)/(A/I)}^{\star})\rightarrow H^{\star}(\Omega_{(B,J)/(A,I)}^{\ast},\mathd)

of graded
\varphi_{(A,I)}^{\ast}(B/J)
-algebras (where
\star
is the
grading), then we deduce that this is in fact an isomorphism. Our presentation
is adapted from the proof of
[
Kat70
, Thm 7.2]
.

\mathord{\star}=0
:

This is the composite map
\varphi_{(A,I)}^{\ast}(B/J)\rightarrow B\rightarrow H^{0}(\Omega_{(B,J)/(A,I)}^{\ast},\mathd)
, i.e., the
\varphi_{(A,I)}^{\ast}(B/J)
-algebra
structure on
H^{0}(\Omega_{(B,J)/(A,I)},\mathd)
.

\mathord{\star}=1
:

Consider the map
B\rightarrow H^{1}(\Omega_{(B,J)/(A,I)}^{\ast},\mathd)
of sets given by
f\mapsto[f^{p-1}\mathd f]
. We first check that this map is additive: in

\Omega_{\mathbb{Z}[u,v]/\mathbb{Z}}^{1}
, we have

\displaystyle(u+v)^{p-1}\mathd(u+v)-u^{p-1}\mathd u-v^{p-1}\mathd v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}(\mathd((u+v)^{p})-\mathd(u^{p})-\mathd(v^{p}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\mathd\left(\sum_{j=1}^{p-1}\binom{p}{j}u^{j}v^{p-1-j}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathd\left(\sum_{j=1}^{p-1}\frac{1}{p}\binom{p}{j}u^{j}v^{p-1-j}\right)

We deduce the additivity by the map
\mathbb{Z}[u,v]\rightarrow B,u\mapsto f,v\mapsto g
.

Now we note that the map
f\mapsto[f^{p-1}\mathd f]
satisfies Leibniz
rule (recall that
H^{1}(\Omega_{(B,J)/(A,I)}^{\ast},\mathd)
is a

\varphi_{(A,I)}^{\ast}(B/J)
-module, therefore a
B/J
-module).
Indeed,
[(fg)^{p-1}\mathd(fg)]=f^{p}[g^{p-1}\mathd g]+g^{p}[f^{p-1}\mathd f]
.

Thus we get a derivation
B/J\rightarrow H^{1}(\Omega_{(B,J)/(A,I)}^{\ast},\mathd)
, which gives rise to a
B/J
-linear map
\Omega_{(B/J)/(A/I)}^{1}\rightarrow H^{1}(\Omega_{(B,J)/(A,I)}^{\ast},\mathd)

and after linearization, we get
\varphi_{(A,I)}^{\ast}\Omega_{(B/J)/(A,I)}^{1}\rightarrow H^{1}(\Omega_{(B,J)/(A,I)}^{\ast},\mathd)
.

\mathord{\star}>1
:

Taking the exterior power of the map for

\mathord{\star}=1
.

Now we show the Cartier isomorphism:

Lemma 4.49
.

Let
(A,I,\gamma)\rightarrow(B,J,\delta)
be an element in

\tmop{CrysCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{0}
. Then the inverse Cartier map
C^{-1}\of\varphi_{(A,I)}^{\ast}(\Omega_{(B/J)/(A/I)}^{\star})\rightarrow H^{\star}(\Omega_{(B,J)/(A,I)}^{\ast},\mathd)
is an isomorphism of
graded
\varphi_{(A,I)}^{\ast}(B/J)
-algebras.

Proof 4.50
.

Recall that
(B,J,\delta)
is of the form
(\Gamma_{A[X]}(Y)\twoheadrightarrow(A/I)[X],\delta)
. It is then direct to check that
the inverse Cartier map
C^{-1}
factors as
\varphi_{(A,I)}^{\ast}(\Omega_{(B/J)/(A/I)}^{\star})\rightarrow H^{\star}(\Omega_{(A[X],IA[X])/(A,I)}^{\ast},\mathd)\rightarrow H^{\star}(\Omega_{(B,J)/(A,I)}^{\ast},\mathd)
, where the first map is the inverse Cartier map
associated to
(A\twoheadrightarrow A/I,\gamma)\rightarrow(A[X],IA[X],\gamma)
, and the second map is an isomorphism by the divided power
Poincaré’s lemma

[
Sta21
,
Tag
07LC
]
.

Thus we can assume that
(B,J,\delta)=(A[X],IA[X],\gamma)
. In this
case, the inverse Cartier map is base-changed from that for
(A,0,0)\rightarrow(A[X],0,0)
along
(A,0,0)\rightarrow(A,I,\gamma)
,
thus we can assume that
I=0
, which is
[
Kat70
, Thm 7.2]
.

It then follows from
Proposition

A.14
that

Proposition 4.51
.

There exists a natural isomorphism
24
24

24

To avoid the ambiguity of
symbols, we suppress the asterisk on
\tmop{Fil}^{\ast}
to avoid confusion
with the pullback symbol
\varphi^{\ast}
.

C^{-1}\of\varphi_{(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}^{\ast}\left(\bigwedgestar_{B^{\prime\prime}}^{\star}L_{B^{\prime\prime}/A^{\prime\prime}}\right)\left[-\mathord{\star}\right]\rightarrow\tmop{gr}_{\tmop{conj}}^{-\mathord{\star}}\tmop{dR}_{(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}

in
\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{Gr}^{\leq 0}(D(\varphi_{A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}}^{\ast}(B^{\prime\prime}))))
, called the
\tmdfn
derived Cartier isomorphism (cf.

[
Bha12a
, Prop 3.5]
), which is functorial
25
25

25

Here we use the
same convention as in
Remark

3.76
.
in
((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\rightarrow(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime},\delta))\in\tmop{dRCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
.

Note that both functors are
(\tmop{dRCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\rightarrow\tmop{CrysCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}})
-invariant (
Definition

2.64
), it follows from

Proposition

2.63
that

Proposition 4.52
.

There exists a natural isomorphism

C^{-1}\of\varphi_{(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}^{\ast}\left(\bigwedgestar_{R}^{\star}L_{R/A^{\prime\prime}}\right)\left[-\mathord{\star}\right]\rightarrow\tmop{gr}_{\tmop{conj}}^{-\mathord{\star}}\tmop{CrysCoh}_{R/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}

in
\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{Gr}^{\leq 0}(D(\varphi_{A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}}^{\ast}(R))))
, called the
\tmdfn
derived Cartier isomorphism, which
is functorial in
((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma),A^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow R)\in\tmop{CrysCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
.

4.3.
Relative animated PD-envelope

Recall that, in the classical case

[
Sta21
,
Tag
07H9
]
, given a PD-pair
(A,I,\gamma)\in\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma}
, and an ideal
J
of
A
containing
I
, there is a
relative PD-envelope
D_{(A,I,\gamma)}(J)
which is essentially the same as

D_{A}(J)\otimes_{D_{A}(I)}A
, where the map
D_{A}(I)\rightarrow A
is
induced by the PD-structure
\gamma
on
A
. We seek an animated version of
this construction, which is needed to study the derived crystalline
cohomology. Roughly speaking, we first construct a relative PD-envelope for
any animated PD-pair
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{A})
along with a map

(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})
of
animated pairs, then we restrict to the special case that the map
A\rightarrow B
is an equivalence. It turns out that the general case can be
recovered from the special case.

Similar to the classical case, the relative animated PD-envelope of
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{A})
along with
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})
is obtained by the base change

\tmop{Env}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})\amalg_{\tmop{Env}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{A})
of the (absolute) animated PD-envelope,
where the map
\tmop{Env}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{A})
is induced by the
PD-structure
\gamma_{A}
. More formally, we start with reviewing the formal
category-theoretic fact about relative adjunctions:

Lemma 4.53

(dual to
[
Lur09
, Prop 5.2.5.1]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\underset{G}{\overset{F}{\longrightleftarrows}}\mathcal{D}
be an adjoint
pair of
\infty
-categories. Assume that the
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}

admits pushouts and let
D\in\mathcal{D}
be an object. Then

(1)

The induced functor
g\of\mathcal{D}_{D\mathord{/}}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{GD\mathord{/}}
admits a left adjoint
f
.

(2)

The functor
f
is equivalent to the composition

\mathcal{C}_{GD\mathord{/}}\xrightarrow{f^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}_{FGD\mathord{/}}\xrightarrow{f^{\prime\prime}}\mathcal{D}_{D\mathord{/}}

where
f^{\prime}
is induced by
F
and
f^{\prime\prime}
is induced by the pushout along the
counit map
FGD\rightarrow D
.

We note that this construction is functorial in
D\in\mathcal{D}
.

{notation}

We denote the comma category
\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}\times_{\tmop{Pair}^{\tmop{an}},\tmop{ev}_{[0]}}\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\tmop{Pair}^{\tmop{an}})
by
\tmop{PDEnvCon}
, an object of which is
denoted by
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\rightarrow(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})
, instead of the cumbersome notation
((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma),(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime}))
.

Definition 4.54
.

Let
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\in\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}
be an animated PD-pair. The

\tmdfn
(relative) animated PD-envelope of an animated pair in

\tmop{Pair}^{\tmop{an}}_{(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})\mathord{/}}
is the
image under the functor
\tmop{Pair}^{\tmop{an}}_{(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})\mathord{/}}\rightarrow\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}_{(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\mathord{/}}
induced by the animated
PD-envelope functor
\tmop{Pair}^{\tmop{an}}\rightarrow\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}
by
Lemma

4.53
.

Concretely, let
B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime}
be an object in

\tmop{Pair}^{\tmop{an}}_{(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})\mathord{/}}
. Then the
relative animated PD-envelope of
B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime}
is given by

\tmop{Env}_{(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{A})}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})\assign\tmop{Env}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})\amalg_{\tmop{Env}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})}(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma_{A})

where the map
\tmop{Env}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)
is the counit map associated
to
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\in\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}

and the map
\tmop{Env}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow\tmop{Env}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})
is
the image of
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})
under the animated PD-envelope functor.

This defines the
\tmdfn
(relative) animated PD-envelope functor

\tmop{PDEnvCon}\rightarrow\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}),((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\rightarrow(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime}))\mapsto\tmop{Env}_{(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})
.

Example 4.55
.

Let
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\in\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}

be an animated PD-pair. Then the animated PD-envelope of
A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}
relative to
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)
is
given by
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)
. This follows from the fact
that
A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}
relative to
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)
is the base change of
\tmop{id}_{\mathbb{Z}}\of\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
relative to
(\tmop{id}_{\mathbb{Z}}\of\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z},0)
along the map
(\tmop{id}_{\mathbb{Z}}\of\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z},0)\rightarrow(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)
of animated PD-pairs. Compare with

Lemma

4.63
.

Example 4.56
.

Let
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\in\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}

be an animated PD-pair. Then the animated PD-envelope of
\tmop{id}_{A^{\prime\prime}}\of A^{\prime\prime}\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}
relative to
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)
is given by
(\tmop{id}_{A^{\prime\prime}}\of A^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime},0)
. This
follows from checking the universal property of the unit map at

\tmop{id}_{A^{\prime\prime}}\of A^{\prime\prime}\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}
of the adjunction

\tmop{Pair}^{\tmop{an}}_{(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})\mathord{/}}\rightleftarrows\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}_{(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\mathord{/}}
.

It follows immediately from
Lemma

3.50
that

Lemma 4.57
.

Let
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\in\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}
be an animated PD-pair,
(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})\in\tmop{Pair}^{\tmop{an}}_{(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime})\mathord{/}}
an animated pair under
A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}
. Let

(C\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime},\delta)
denote its relative animated
PD-envelope. Then the unit map
(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow(C\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})
becomes an equivalence after rationalization.

Recall that given a PD-pair
(A,I,\gamma)
and a map
(A,I)\rightarrow(B,J)
of pairs with
A\rightarrow B
being flat, the PD-structure
\gamma

extends to
B
, i.e, there exists a unique PD-structure
\overline{\gamma}
on

(B,IB)
such that the map
(A,I)\rightarrow(B,J)
of pairs gives rise to
a map
(A,I,\gamma)\rightarrow(B,IB,\overline{\gamma})
of PD-pairs.
Then the PD-envelope of
(B,J)
with respect to
(A,I,\gamma)
is the same
as that with respect to the PD-pair
(B,IB,\overline{\gamma})
, which
corresponds to the crystalline cohomology of
B/J
with respect to
(B,IB,\overline{\gamma})
. We now show an animated analogue (without flatness).

{notation}

Let
\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}
denote the full subcategory

\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}\times_{\tmop{CAlg}^{\tmop{an}}}\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\tmop{CAlg}^{\tmop{an}})_{\geq 0}\subseteq\tmop{CrysCon}

spanned by objects
((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma),A^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow R)

such that
A^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow R
is also surjective.

\construction

There is a
canonical functor
\tmop{PDEnvCon}\rightarrow\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}

given as follows: for every object
((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\rightarrow(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime}))\in\tmop{PDEnvCon}
, we get the
commutative diagram

\begin{array}[]{ccc}A&\nonconverted{longtwoheadrightarrow}&A^{\prime\prime}\\
\longdownarrow&&\longdownarrow\\
B&\nonconverted{longtwoheadrightarrow}&B^{\prime\prime}\end{array}

in
\tmop{CAlg}^{\tmop{an}}
, which gives rise to two surjective maps
B\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}A^{\prime\prime}\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime}
and
B\twoheadrightarrow B\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}A^{\prime\prime}
. Furthermore, the later admits a PD-structure:
it is the underlying animated pair of the pushout
(\tmop{id}_{B}\of B\rightarrow B,0)\amalg_{(\tmop{id}_{A}\of A\rightarrow A,0)}(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)
in
\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}
. We
denote by
(B\twoheadrightarrow B\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}A^{\prime\prime},\delta)
this
pushout. Then we get an object
((B\twoheadrightarrow B\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}A^{\prime\prime},\delta),B\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}A^{\prime\prime}\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})
in
\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}
.

One verifies that

Lemma 4.58
.

The functor
\tmop{PDEnvCon}\rightarrow\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}
in
Construction
4.3
admits a fully
faithful right adjoint
\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}\rightarrow\tmop{PDEnvCon}
given by
((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma),A^{\prime\prime}\twoheadrightarrow R)\mapsto((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\rightarrow(A\twoheadrightarrow R))
.

Thus
\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}
could be seen as a reflective subcategory
(
Definition

2.52
) of
\tmop{PDEnvCon}
. Now we claim that

Lemma 4.59
.

The relative animated PD-envelope functor

\tmop{PDEnvCon}\rightarrow\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}})
is
(\tmop{PDEnvCon}\rightarrow\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}})
-invariant (
Definition

2.64
).

Proof 4.60
.

For every object
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\rightarrow(B\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime\prime})
in
\tmop{PDEnvCon}
, we have a map
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\rightarrow(B\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}B,\delta)
of animated PD-pairs. By the concrete
description of the relative animated PD-envelope functor, it suffices to
show that this map along with the counit maps forms a pushout diagram of
animated PD-pairs. As discussed in
Construction
4.3
,
(B\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}B,\delta)
is the pushout

(\tmop{id}_{B}\of B\rightarrow B,0)\amalg_{(\tmop{id}_{A}\of A\rightarrow A,0)}(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)
. The counit maps for

(\tmop{id}_{A},0)
and
(\tmop{id}_{B},0)
are identities
(
Lemma

3.47
). The result then follows from

Proposition

3.40
, which implies that counit maps are
compatible with small colimits.

Consequently, in order to study the relative animated PD-envelope functor, it
suffices to study the composite
\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}\rightarrow\tmop{PDEnvCon}\rightarrow\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}})
. By abuse of terminology, we will simply denote this functor as

\tmop{RelPDEnv}
as well and call the image (or after evaluation at
1\in\Delta^{1}
)
\tmdfn
the animated PD-envelope of an object
((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma),A^{\prime\prime}\twoheadrightarrow R)\in\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}
. We remark that the functor

\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}\rightarrow\tmop{PDEnvCon}
preserves small
colimits by
Proposition

3.40
, therefore so does the composite
functor.

We note that
\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}
is projectively generated: let

\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}^{0}\subseteq\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}
be the
full subcategory spanned by objects
((\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}[Y,Z]}(X)\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}[Y,Z],\gamma),\mathbb{Z}[Y,Z]\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}[Z])
for all finite sets
X,Y,Z
.

Lemma 4.61
.

The full subcategory

\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}^{0}\subseteq\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}

constitutes a set of compact projective generators for

\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}
.

Proof 4.62
.

We only sketch the proof, which is similar to that of

Theorem

3.31
. The key observation is that the composite of
forgetful functors
\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}^{0}\rightarrow\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{2},\tmop{CAlg}^{\tmop{an}})_{\tmop{surj}}\rightarrow\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{2},D(\mathbb{Z})_{\geq 0})_{\tmop{surj}},((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma),A^{\prime\prime}\twoheadrightarrow R)\mapsto(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}\twoheadrightarrow R)
, which preserves filtered colimits and geometric
realizations by
Proposition

3.40
, admits a left adjoint,
where
\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{2},\mathcal{C})_{\tmop{surj}}\subseteq\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{2},\mathcal{C})
is the full subcategory spanned by
(X\rightarrow Y\rightarrow Z)\in\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{2},\mathcal{C})
such that
X\rightarrow Y
and
Y\rightarrow Z
are surjective, for
\mathcal{C}=D(\mathbb{Z})_{\geq 0}
and
\mathcal{C}=\tmop{CAlg}^{\tmop{an}}
.

The
\infty
-category
\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{2},D(\mathbb{Z})_{\geq 0})_{\tmop{surj}}
admits a set
\{\mathbb{Z}X\oplus\mathbb{Z}Y\oplus\mathbb{Z}Z\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}Y\oplus\mathbb{Z}Z\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}Z\barsuchthat X,Y,Z\in\tmop{Fin}\}
of
compact projective generators which spans the full subcategory
\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{2},D(\mathbb{Z})_{\geq 0})_{\tmop{surj}}^{0}
, which follows from the
fact that the left adjoint to the left derived functor
\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{2},D(\mathbb{Z})_{\geq 0})_{\tmop{surj}}^{0})\rightarrow\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{2},D(\mathbb{Z})_{\geq 0})_{\tmop{surj}}^{0}
is
conservative (cf. the proof of
[
Lur17
, Prop 25.2.1.2]
).

The result then follows from
Proposition

A.19
.

By
Proposition

A.14
, the functor
\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}\rightarrow\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}})
is the
left derived functor of the restricted functor
\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}^{0}\rightarrow\tmop{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}})
, which is
concretely given as follows:

Lemma 4.63
.

The relative animated PD-envelope of an
object
((\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}[Y,Z]}(X)\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}[Y,Z],\gamma),\mathbb{Z}[Y,Z]\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}[Z])\in\tmop{CrysCon}_{\tmop{surj}}^{0}
is functorially given by

(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}[Z]}(X,Y)\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}[Z],\tilde{\gamma})\in\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}_{(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}[Y,Z]}(X)\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}[Y,Z],\gamma)\mathord{/}}
, i.e.
coincides with the classical relative PD-envelope.

Proof 4.64
.

First, by the adjointness, there exists a functorial comparison map from the
relative animated PD-envelope to
(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}[Z]}(X,Y)\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}[Z],\tilde{\gamma})
. It suffices to show
that this is an equivalence.

In this case,
((\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}[Y,Z]}(X)\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}[Y,Z],\gamma),\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}[Y,Z]}(X)\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}[Z])\in\tmop{PDEnvCon}
is the base change
of
((\tmop{id}_{\mathbb{Z}[Y,Z]},0),\mathbb{Z}[Y,Z]\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}[Z])\in\tmop{PDEnvCon}
along

(\tmop{id}_{\mathbb{Z}[Y,Z]},0)\rightarrow(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}[Y,Z]}(X)\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}[Y,Z],\gamma)
. The result then follows
from the base-change property of the relative adjunction, along with the
simple fact that the (absolute) animated PD-envelope of
\mathbb{Z}[Y,Z]\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}[Z]
is
(\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}[Z]}(Y)\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}[Z],\gamma)
.

As a generalization of
Definition

3.72
, we now introduce the

\tmdfn
conjugate filtration on the relative animated PD-envelope in

\tmop{char}p
.

\construction

Let
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)\in\tmop{Pair}^{\gamma}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
be a PD-pair and
I\subseteq A^{\prime\prime}

Remark 4.70
.

More precisely, in

Corollary

3.82
, the map
\varphi_{A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}}^{\ast}(R)\rightarrow B
is induced by the Frobenius map
\varphi_{B\twoheadrightarrow R}\of R\rightarrow B
(which could be seen by left
deriving the special case that
((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma),A^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow R)\in\tmop{CrysCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p},\tmop{surj}}^{0}
). In
particular, if the Frobenius map
\varphi_{A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}}\of A^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow A
is flat, then so is the Frobenius map
\varphi_{B\twoheadrightarrow R}\of R\rightarrow B
.

For example, when
R
is a quasiregular semiperfect
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebra

[
BMS19
, Def 8.8]
, we set
(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)=(\tmop{id}_{R^{\flat}}\of R^{\flat}\rightarrow R^{\flat},0)
and the map

A^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow R
to be the canonical map, by definition,
R^{\flat}
is a
perfect
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebra therefore
\varphi_{R^{\flat}}
is flat. Then
the animated PD-envelope
B\twoheadrightarrow R
of
A^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow R

satisfies the condition that the Frobenius map
\varphi_{B\rightarrow R}\of R\rightarrow B
is flat and hence
B
is static. It follows that
(R\twoheadrightarrow B,\delta)
is a PD-pair
(
Proposition

3.38
).

Note that the associated graded pieces of derived crystalline cohomology and
relative animated PD-envelope of a “surjective” crystalline context
((A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma),A^{\prime\prime}\twoheadrightarrow R)\in\tmop{CrysCon}_{\mathbb{F}_{p},\tmop{surj}}
, with respect to conjugate
filtrations, are equivalent by

4.67
,
4.52
and
3.64
. In
fact, we have

Lemma 4.71
.

There is a canonical equivalence

\tmop{Fil}_{\tmop{conj}}\tmop{CrysCoh}_{R/(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)}\rightarrow\tmop{Fil}_{\tmop{conj}}\tmop{Env}_{(A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime},\gamma)}^{\gamma,\tmop{an}}(R)

in
\tmop{CAlg}(\tmop{DF}^{\leq 0}(\varphi_{A\twoheadrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}}^{\ast}(R)))
which is functorial
28
28

28

Here we apply the same
convention as in

.
in
